证券研究报告 中航证券研究所发布 
股市有风险   入市需谨慎

[Table_summary] 报告摘要 
◆ 进口显著提速，前10个月外贸增长稳中有增 
以美元计价，10月我国进出口同比+37.90%，其中出口+35.20%、进
口+41.60%，均超 Wind一致预期，单月规模均创历史同期新高，进出口
和进口增速均创年内新高，出口增速较 9月小幅回落但仍处高位；前 10
个月出口、进口累计分别+22.70%、+29.00%，均为 2022年以来同期最
高。出口方面，机电产品拉动+28.75PCTs，较 9月进一步抬升，集成电
路、自动数据处理设备及其零部件、汽车仍是主要拉动，但集成电路和
汽车拉动边际回落；当月八类重点商品量价齐升，范围较 9 月有所收
窄。进口方面，机电产品拉动+23.47PCTs，其中集成电路、自动数据处
理设备及其零部件分别拉动+13.64PCTs、+8.04PCTs，原油拉动明显增
强，进口支撑进一步扩展至能源产品。 
分目的地看，10月东盟、中国香港、美国位列当月拉动前三；前 10
个月东盟、中国香港、欧盟位列累计拉动前三，出口目的地结构总体稳
定，市场多元化格局保持稳定。外部景气看，10月有 7个制造业 PMI处
于扩张区间，较 9月减少 1个，外需仍有一定支撑，但扩张面有所收
窄，区域分化仍在延续。 
◆ 政策预期趋稳，全年外贸有望保持韧性 
10月，我国外贸运行呈现出口在高位小幅回落、进口增速明显提升
的态势。整体来看，10月我国外贸主要呈现以下特征：一是外贸总量延
续高位运行，进口提速对冲了出口增速的小幅回落；二是机电产品仍是
双向贸易的重要支撑，但出口核心品类和量价表现趋于分化，进口支撑
进一步扩展至能源品；三是出口目的地结构总体稳定，外部景气扩张面
收窄与政策预期改善并存。 
政策层面，中美举行第八轮经贸磋商，双方同意将吉隆坡经贸磋商
联合安排延期至 2027年 1月 10日，部分关税和出口管制措施继续暂
停，有助于延长政策缓冲期、稳定企业预期；同期，双方在贸易理事会
框架下就各自 300亿美元规模的对等降税安排达成共识，为相关商品贸
易改善提供了政策条件。11月 9日，商务部、海关总署进一步明确相关
出口管制措施自 11月 11日起继续暂停至 2027年 1月 10日，年内外贸
政策环境的可预期性进一步增强。整体来看，近期经贸安排以稳定存量
政策环境和管控分歧为主，政策预期趋稳或对年内外贸形成边际支撑。 
展望年内，虽然主要经济体制造业景气扩张面有所收窄，外需动能
或存在波动风险，但我国电子产业链相关贸易仍将维持活跃态势，外贸
核心支撑基本面未发生改变；进口增长的拉动来源进一步拓宽，出口市
场结构总体保持稳定，政策预期边际趋稳有望继续降低外贸企业经营不
确定性。整体来看，我国年内外贸仍具备保持韧性的基础，全年外贸以
较高增速收官的基础仍在，但年内后续出口或呈高位波动格局；进口在
电子零部件、能源品的支撑下有望维持较快增长。 
风险提示：全球经济超预期走弱；地缘政治事件烈度持续升级

### Page 2

中航证券研究所发布 
一、进口显著提速，前 10个月外贸增长稳中有增 
以美元计价，10月我国进出口金额 7193.83亿美元，同比+37.90%。其中，出口
同比+35.20%，高于 Wind一致预期的+33.25%；进口同比+41.60%，高于 Wind一致预期
的+30.36%，分别高出预期 1.95个和 11.24个 PCTs。前 10个月，我国进出口金额
65229.29 亿美元，同比+25.30%，其中出口同比+22.70%，进口同比+29.00%。单月来
看，10月进出口和进口同比增速较 9月分别上升 3.80个和 10.50个 PCTs，均创年内
新高；出口增速较 9月回落 1.10个 PCTs，但仍处较高水平。累计来看，前 10个月进
出口、出口和进口增速均为 2022年以来同期最高，外贸增长呈现出稳中有增的态势。 
以美元计价，10月贸易差额 1052.77亿美元，较去年同期的 879.98亿美元增加
172.79亿美元，同比+19.64%；前 10个月累计贸易差额 10425.94亿美元，较去年同
期的 9583.73亿美元增加 842.21亿美元，同比+8.79%。以人民币计价，前 10个月贸
易差额 71632.65亿元，较去年同期的 68738.84亿元增加 2893.80亿元，同比+4.21%。 
图表 1：以美元计价进出口金额当月同比数据(%)

资料来源：wind，中航证券研究所 
10月出口产品结构延续机电产品主导的特征，但核心品类拉动有所分化。具体来
看，2026年 10月机电产品对出口同比增速的拉动为+28.75PCTs，较 9月的+27.63PCTs
上升 1.12个 PCTs，仍是出口增长的主要支撑（当月拉动=（该项目当月出口金额-该
项目去年同期出口金额）/去年同期总出口金额）。其中，集成电路、自动数据处理设
备及其零部件、汽车和汽车底盘的拉动分别为+7.51PCTs、+6.12PCTs和+1.77PCTs，较
9月分别回落 0.39个 PCTs、上升 0.98个 PCTs和回落 0.65个 PCTs，自动数据处理设
备及其零部件的拉动力的提升在一定程度上对冲了另外两类商品拉动的回落。从新型
产业看，高新技术产品对出口同比增速的拉动为+18.15PCTs，较 9月上升 0.72个 PCTs。
传统产业中，水产品的拉动为+0.04PCTs；纺织纱线、织物及其制品的拉动为+0.56PCTs，
较 9月回落 0.14个 PCTs。整体来看，上述传统商品对出口增速的拉动总体较为有限，
本月出口增长仍主要由机电产品支撑。 
进口方面，10月机电产品对进口同比增速的拉动为+23.47PCTs，较 9月上升 1.82
个 PCTs，仍是进口增长的首要支撑。其中，集成电路和自动数据处理设备及其零部件
的拉动力分别为+13.64PCTs和+8.04PCTs，分别较 9月上升 0.83个 PCTs和-0.91个
PCTs。此外，原油对进口的拉动由9月的+0.33PCTs升至+4.58PCTs，上升4.25个PCTs，
从量价数据看，10月原油进口价格同比涨幅较 9月扩大，进口数量同比降幅收窄，共
同推动原油进口金额同比增速明显回升。机电产品在我国进、出口两端的拉动同步上

### Page 3

中航证券研究所发布 
升，一定程度上反映我国电子信息产业链相关贸易仍较活跃；同时，原油拉动明显增
强，10月进口支撑由电子产业链进一步扩展至能源产品。 
从出口量价关系看，10月成品油、稀土、箱包及类似容器、鞋靴、钢材、未锻造
的铝及铝材、家用电器、汽车和汽车底盘八类重点商品实现量价齐升，较 9月减少两
类。从重点跟踪商品看，集成电路出口价格同比涨幅最大，为+143.18%；汽车和汽车
底盘出口数量同比涨幅最大，为+33.53%。前 10个月累计来看，箱包及类似容器、未
锻造的铝及铝材、家用电器、集成电路、汽车和汽车底盘五类商品实现量价齐升，其
中集成电路出口价格同比+104.43%，汽车和汽车底盘出口数量同比+49.50%。10 月量
价齐升商品范围较 9月有所收窄，部分商品价格上升并未伴随数量同步增长，商品出
口增长的价格与数量贡献仍有明显分化。 
分出口目的地看，10月我国对东盟、中国香港和美国出口的拉动分别为+8.10PCTs、
+4.90PCTs和+3.77PCTs，位列当月前三，欧盟和韩国分别以+3.50PCTs和+3.27PCTs
位列第四、第五。拉动力变化情况来看，10 月各出口对象对出口同比增速的拉动力
整体波动较小，市场结构与 9月总体接近。在我们跟踪的 15个出口目的地中，14个
目的地的拉动较 9月变动幅度不超过 0.60个 PCTs，变动幅度最大的出口目的地为韩
国，拉动较 9月上升 0.72个 PCTs。10月我国对东盟、欧盟、中国香港、美国和拉丁
美洲五大市场的出口占比分别为 18.91%、13.24%、11.08%、11.26%和 7.73%。前 10
个月累计来看，我国对东盟、中国香港和欧盟出口的累计拉动分别为+5.35PCTs、
+4.35PCTs和+2.57PCTs，位列累计前三，韩国和非洲分别以+1.68PCTs和+1.40PCTs
位列第四、第五；对美出口的累计拉动升至+1.38PCTs，延续 6月转正以来的修复态
势。从出口结构占比看，前 10个月我国对东盟、欧盟、中国香港、美国和拉丁美洲
五大市场的出口占比分别为 18.66%、14.28%、10.67%、10.43%和 7.57%；与 2025年
全年相比，对东盟、中国香港出口占比分别上升 1.02个和 1.76个 PCTs，对美国、
欧盟和拉丁美洲出口占比分别回落 0.71个、0.58个和 0.32个 PCTs，中国香港出口
占比继续高于美国。整体来看，10 月出口目的地格局未出现明显切换，东盟和中国
香港仍是主要支撑，对美出口累计拉动继续修复，市场多元化格局总体保持稳定。 
从外部景气度看，我们监测的 10个主要经济体中（欧元区、日本、韩国、美国、
加拿大、俄罗斯、巴西、印度、南非和东盟，东盟作为整体看待），10月有 7个制造
业 PMI处于扩张区间，较 9月减少 1个。处于扩张区间的经济体中，美国 ISM制造业
PMI录得 55.5，较 9月回升 1.0个点；南非录得 55.6，较 9月回升 3.6个点；东盟
录得 53.1，较 9月回升 1.0个点。欧元区、日本、韩国和印度仍处扩张区间，但分
别录得 52.7、53.3、51.3和 54.7，较 9月分别回落 0.2个、0.8个、2.6个和 0.4
个点。加拿大录得 49.6，较 9月回落 1.9个点，由扩张区间转入收缩区间；俄罗斯
和巴西分别录得 49.9和 46.1，虽较 9月分别回升 0.1个和 1.3个点，但仍处收缩区
间。整体来看，主要经济体制造业景气仍有一定支撑，但扩张面有所收窄，外需动能
的区域分化仍在延续。

中航证券研究所发布 
二、政策预期趋稳，外贸韧性有望延续 
10月，我国外贸运行呈现出口在高位小幅回落、进口增速明显提升的态势。整体
来看，10 月我国外贸主要呈现以下特征：一是外贸总量延续高位运行，进口提速对
冲了出口增速的小幅回落；二是机电产品仍是双向贸易的重要支撑，但出口核心品类
和量价表现趋于分化，进口支撑进一步扩展至能源品；三是出口目的地结构总体稳定，
外部景气扩张面收窄与政策预期改善并存。 
政策层面，9月 20日至 23日，中美举行第八轮经贸磋商；据商务部 9月 24日介
绍，双方同意将吉隆坡经贸磋商联合安排延期至 2027年 1月 10日，部分关税和出
口管制措施继续暂停，有助于延长政策缓冲期、稳定企业预期；同期，双方在贸易理
事会框架下就各自 300 亿美元规模的对等降税安排达成共识，为相关商品贸易改善
提供了政策条件。11月 9日，商务部、海关总署进一步明确相关出口管制措施自 11
月11日起继续暂停至 2027年 1月 10日，年内外贸政策环境的可预期性进一步增强。
整体来看，近期经贸安排以稳定存量政策环境和管控分歧为主，政策预期趋稳或对年
内外贸形成边际支撑。 
展望年内，虽然主要经济体制造业景气扩张面有所收窄，外需动能或存在波动风
险，但我国电子产业链相关贸易仍将维持活跃态势，外贸核心支撑基本面未发生改变；
进口增长的拉动来源进一步拓宽，出口市场结构总体保持稳定，政策预期边际趋稳有
望继续降低外贸企业经营不确定性。整体来看，我国年内外贸仍具备保持韧性的基础，
全年外贸以较高增速收官的基础仍在，但年内后续出口或呈高位波动格局；进口在电
子零部件、能源品的支撑下有望维持较快增长。

中航证券研究所发布

公司的投资评级如下： 
买入：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数涨幅10%以上。 
增持：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数涨幅5%~10%之间。 
持有：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数涨幅-10%~+5%之间。 
卖出：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数跌幅10%以上。

行业的投资评级如下： 
增持：未来六个月行业增长水平高于同期沪深300指数。 
中性：未来六个月行业增长水平与同期沪深300指数相若。 
减持：未来六个月行业增长水平低于同期沪深300指数。

研究团队介绍汇总：  
中航证券宏观团队：立足国内，放眼国际，全面覆盖国内外宏观经济、政策研究。以自上而下的宏观视角，诠释经济运行趋势与规律，以把握流动性变化为
核心，指导大类资产配置。

King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific

Feedback
Continuing capacity build with unchanged 2027 demand: The company mentioned
that AI demand remains strong and customers (especially the largest customer) are
still asking for more clean room build. 2027 demand is almost confirmed with no
changes over the past month.
Capacity allocation: US capacity will fully support the largest customer initially and
the capacity in Taiwan is fungible between different customers. For example, given

4Q26 weakness for consumer/smartphones, the company has shifted spare capacity
to meet other demand, including the testers from Advantest and Teradyne.
Margin should still be in the upward trend: The priority for the company now is to
have customers consign the equipment if they are asking for more capacity, lowering
the deprecation pressure. Also, given the tight capacity, the company would serve
the products with higher margin (generally AI demand and longer test time product)

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

12-18 months.

張予柔 yurou.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

貿聯-KY (3665 TT) 
併購綜效超預期，獲利動能再加速

永豐觀點 
併購 Interplex、新富生貢獻雙雙超預期，獲利上修循環啟動。 
 投資評價與建議 
重申買進建議：研究部本次上修貿聯財務預估：(1) Interplex 資料通訊業務
(Datacom Business)營收規模大幅超標：10 月首個完整併表月營收約新台幣 21
億元，大幅優於研究部原先預期之約 13 億元，研究部同步上修其 2026~2028 年
營收與獲利貢獻預估。(2) 新富生月營收規模自年初以來已成長逾一倍，憑藉資料
中心高階光纖跳線的製造與品質優勢，持續拓展新客戶，研究部預估其 2027 年營
收可持續倍增。(3) 產品組合轉佳、營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 毛利率季增 1.8 個
百點至 32.53%，且營業費用率降至約 12%，帶動營益率創 20.78%新高。綜合預
估貿聯 2026-2028 年集團營收分別為 1,074、1,659、2,161 億元(+51%、+55%、
+30%YoY)；稅後淨利 150、267、356 元(+66%、+79%、+33%YoY)，完全稀
釋後 EPS 分別為 75.25、134.07 及 178.24 元。考量 AI 供應鏈遇短期逆風，影響
評價表現，研究部將本益比調整為 18 倍，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027
年及 2028 年之平均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

ESG 評析 
貿聯企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。

### Page 2

2

貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：
貿聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、
工業用 31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設
備(SPE)占 13％、其他事業占 41％。貿聯主要核心能力，在於高技術及客製化開
發零組件製造，產品開發以多角化及差異化策略，2026/1 完成收購新富生光電
(XFS)，2026/9/23 完成收購 Interplex 集團資料通訊業務(Interplex Datacom)，
產品線由電源與數據連接延伸至光纖跳線、機殼及機櫃等精密機構件，朝 AI 機櫃
次系統解決方案供應商發展。 
3Q26 財報公布，優於市場預期，營收與獲利同創新高，營益率首度突破 20%：
3Q26 營收 278.75 億元(+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY)，毛利率 32.53%，季增 1.8
個百分點；營業利益率 20.78%，季增 3.6 個百分點、年增 1.2 個百分點，為公司
首度突破 20%，獲利表現優於市場預期。成長主要來自 HPC，3Q26 HPC 營收季
增 30%、年增 61%，營收比重由 2Q26 的 42%回升至 46%，其中電源產品為成
長主力，帶動產品組合改善；半導體設備營收亦季增 11%、年增 70%，各事業全
面成長。費用方面，營收規模擴大但營業費用未等比例增加，營業費用率降至約
12%的歷史低點，顯示獲利率改善來自規模效益與費用效率，而非一次性因素，具
備延續性。稅後淨利 42.93 億元(+44.0% QoQ、+63.3% YoY)，稀釋後 EPS 21.51
元，營收、淨利與 EPS 皆創歷史新高；前三季累計 EPS 48.04 元，已超越 2025 全
年之 46.07 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

研究部上修 2026~2028 年營收獲利預估，原因有三： 
1. Interplex 資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)營收提前兩年達標：Interplex
的資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)自 9/23 起併表，3Q26 僅貢獻 7 天營
收；10 月為首個完整併表月，帶動貿聯 10 月營收達 118.46 億元，月增 26.9%、
年增 80.0%，其中 Interplex 貢獻 6,700 萬美元(約新台幣 21 億元)。對照公司
宣布併購時揭露之年營收 3.92 億美元(月平均約 3,300 萬美元)，Interplex 月
營收已倍增，大幅優於市場預期。研究部看好併購綜效將逐步顯現：Interplex
主要供應 AI 伺服器機殼、機櫃等精密機構件，研究部認為其營收較併購時大
幅成長，主因 AI 機櫃出貨量較年初明顯放大，且併入貿聯後，Interplex 可運
用集團的資金、客戶關係與製造資源擴大接單，雙方產品整合後可提供涵蓋配
電、數據連接、機構件與液冷的機櫃次系統方案，單一 AI 機櫃之可供應產值
明顯擴大。研究部本次上修 2027、2028 年 Interplex Datacom Business 為
貿聯帶來的營收預估，分別為 268、308 億元(原預估 183、250 億元)。 
2. 新富生光通訊規模快速放大，營收占比持續提升：新富生(XFS) 專精於光纖互
連產品的設計與製造，主要產品包括單芯／雙芯跳線、光纖尾纖、多芯光纖以
及 MTP/MPO 高密度連接器，月營收規模自 2026 年初以來成長逾一倍，獲
利能力同步改善，展現規模化高精密光學互連業務的效益。資料中心對光纖組
件的品質與一致性要求遠高於一般應用，新富生在高階光纖跳線的製造經驗與
品質優勢，加上貿聯集團的客戶網絡與全球產能布局，有助其持續拓展新客戶。
隨 AI 資料中心機櫃間光纖連接需求成長，研究部預估新富生 2027 年營收可
望再翻倍增長，且其獲利率改善亦有助提升貿聯整體獲利結構。 
3. 3Q26 營益率創新高，營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 營業費用率約 12%，較 1H26
的 13.7%明顯下降，顯示貿聯在承接更大規模、更複雜的客戶專案時，製造、
工程與行政職能的效率同步提升，費用無須等比例增加。4Q26 起 Interplex
完整併表，營收規模再擴大，研究部預期營益率改善幅度可望大於毛利率，稅
後淨利成長幅度將高於營收。 
HPC 電源成長最強，新平台升級為下一波動能：3Q26 HPC 營收年增 61%、季增
30%，前三季累計年增 77%。隨運算功耗提升，電源產品切入規格更高的新一代
平台；數據連接方面，貿聯同時布局銅纜與光纖，因應不同傳輸距離與頻寬需求。
貿聯於 10 月 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出適用於 OCP 及 NVIDIA MGX 機櫃
之 800V/400VDC 高功率電源方案，以及 1.6T 液冷交換器 OSFP 連接器、機櫃管
理控制與漏液偵測系統，產品範圍由電源線材延伸至配電、液冷與監控。研究部認
為，隨 Vera Rubin 平台放量及 HVDC 架構導入，單一機櫃的電源產品規格與產值
將持續提升，為 2027 年 HPC 成長主軸。 
次世代光通訊布局：貿聯 2026 年先後於 OFC 展示 Optical Shuffle、於 Computex
展示 CPO 交換器托盤方案；9 月於 ECOC 2026 展示與 ams OSRAM 合作之
microVCSEL 光學互連方案，採用 850nm 二維可定址 microVCSEL 陣列，以多通
道、低速率(wide-and-slow)架構降低功耗與延遲，貿聯負責光纖陣列組裝、光學
封裝、光學測試與量產製造；10 月於 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出 CPO、
800G/1.6T 及 XPO 光收發模組、光纖陣列(FAU)與多芯光纖連接等方案。公司表
示產業未來可能並行採用多種光學架構，貿聯以製造與整合能力參與各技術路線，
不需押注單一方案。 
半導體設備成長幅度為產業兩倍以上：3Q26 半導體設備(SPE)營收年增 70%、季
增 11%，營收占比由 3Q25 的 11%提升至 13%，前三季累計年增 48%。根據國
際半導體產業協會（SEMI）於 2026 年 7 月公布之預測，全球前段晶圓製造設備

### Page 4

4

(WFE)市場 2026 年預估年增 23.1%至 1,439 億美元，貿聯 SPE 成長幅度約為產業
的兩倍；自 2023 年以來貿聯之 SPE 業務成長即大幅超越整體 WFE 市場，超額成
長主因貿聯在客戶端的角色由供應線材零組件，擴大至高階組裝、模組與次系統製
造、測試及系統整合，應用以薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備為主，微影與檢測設備為輔；隨
設備製程日益複雜，客戶傾向將更多製造工作委外，以集中資源於核心技術，貿聯
單一設備平台之內含價值隨之提升。SEMI 預估 WFE 市場 2027 年及 2028 年將分
別成長 21.8%及 14.1%，2028 年規模可望突破 2,000 億美元，主要由先進邏輯、
HBM 等先進記憶體投資帶動；公司亦表示，許多客戶對設備投資的強勁預期延伸
至 2030 年以後，且客戶正主動協助貿聯擴充產能，以支援其自身長期成長。貿聯
在 SPE 領域的投資以擴充製造產能為主，Interplex 的精密機械加工與次系統整合
能力亦可與 SPE 業務互補，為 HPC 以外的第二成長引擎。 
隨伺服器架構升級驅動產值倍增，2027~2028 年獲利成長路徑清晰，重申買進： 
研究部認為，貿聯本次財報的關鍵不只是單季獲利優於預期，而是兩項併購同時優
於預期，帶動營收基期結構性墊高：10 月營收 118.46 億元，較 3Q26 月平均約
93 億元高出約 27%。在 AI 機櫃同時需要電源、數據傳輸(銅纜與光纖)與精密機構
件的趨勢下，貿聯透過 Interplex 與新富生補齊產品版圖，單一機櫃可供應產值持
續擴大；而未來一年內，Interplex 完整併表(4Q26)、新富生營收比重提升(2027)、
Vera Rubin 平台與 HVDC 電 源 放 量 (2027~2028) 及 次 世 代 光 互 連 產 品
(2H27~2028)等成長催化劑將陸續到位，獲利上修動能明確。 
評價方面，短期因市場擔憂 GPU 產品推出節奏可能放緩，台灣 AI 供應鏈評價短
期受到壓抑。研究部認為，CSP 資本支出與現行平台訂單短期不受影響，且貿聯的
客戶與產品布局相對分散：公司採取平台中立策略，產品同時供應 GPU 與 ASIC
架構之 AI 機櫃，並服務多家雲端服務業者；銅纜與光纖、電源與機構件等產品線
亦不依賴單一技術路線；此外，半導體設備與其他事業合計仍占營收逾五成，營運
受單一平台影響的程度低於純 GPU 供應鏈。惟考量主要客戶經營層異動可能影響
新平台推出節奏，研究部於評價上給予折價，本益比由前次之 25 倍調整為 18 倍。
搭配本次上修後之 EPS，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027 年及 2028 年之平
均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

3Q26Re: 고난의 행군 너머를 볼 수 있는가

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

목표주가 (하향) 165,000 원 
현재주가 108,600 원

### Page 1

2026년 10월 29일 
기업분석

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(LTA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 LTA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 LTA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 LTA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

### Page 4

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 
본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 자료를 
기관투자자 또는 제3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박준영, 김나우) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 09 월 30 일)

Document 258: AI估值被严重误判！贝莱德基金经理驳斥“见顶论”：Anthropic上市标志着应用层大爆发

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI估值被严重误判！贝莱德基金经理驳斥“见顶论”：Anthropic上市标志着应用层大爆发.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

⻉莱德AI主题ETF基⾦经理Tony Kim认为，市场严重误判了AI估值：芯⽚硬件股盈利⾼增
却已跌⾄低于标普500的⽔平，背后是"2027年⻅顶、2028年下滑"的⼀致预期。模型能⼒
每年提升⼗倍、落地速度远未跟上，AI交易正从算⼒层切向应⽤层，Anthropic融资事件是
分⽔岭；五年后机器流量将占互联⽹99%，Agent⾝份安全与数据层是最先受益的环节。
摘要：
管理着约140亿美元⻉莱德AI主题ETF的Tony Kim，对当下华尔街最流⾏的⼀个判断给出了截然
相反的答案。
他在彭博《Trillions》播客中认为，市场正在为"AI资本开⽀2027年⻅顶、2028年⾛平甚⾄下
滑"定价，直接后果是——芯⽚与硬件公司的盈利在成倍增⻓，股价却在跌，估值已经低于标普
500整体⽔平。"我确实认为市场在向下超调。"
在他看来，这不是估值分歧，⽽是对"变化速率"的系统性低估：模型能⼒每年提升约⼀个数量
级，⽽现实经济的采⽤速度远远落后于这条曲线。
他把Anthropic的⼀级市场融资事件视为分⽔岭——AI交易正从"建算⼒"切换到"跑应⽤"。

芯⽚股⽐⼤盘还便宜，市场在给⼀个"2027⻅顶"的剧本定价
Kim 的第⼀层论证从估值结构切⼊。他把当下的科技板块拆成两块：芯⽚与硬件，以及超⼤
⼚。
原因不在基本⾯，在预期。
他把这称作当前最⼤的分歧点："要么你相信AI会继续下去，那这些东西便宜得离谱；要么你相
信AI会⻅顶回落，那市场的恐惧就是合理的。"
超⼤⼚同样被下杀了。Kim 认为，⾕歌、微软、Meta 这类公司正在把业务的根本重⼼转向算⼒
基础设施提供⽅，"它们把每⼀块钱利润都投进资本开⽀，⾃由现⾦流因此掉下来甚⾄转负，估
值⾃然被打下来"。他的判断是这类公司的资本开⽀还要再烧"两三年"，之后才会迎来回报。
⾄于市场为何在此时杀估值，他归因于融资能⼒与投资回报两个担忧叠加。今年约⼀万亿美元
的资本开⽀中，"85%到90%来⾃最⼤的那⼏家公司，只有10%到15%落在那些资产负债表不够
厚的第⼆梯队新云⼚商⾝上"，⽽这部分恰恰是市场最担⼼的环节，英伟达还是其中最⼤的⽀持
者之⼀。
"这些公司现在的估值⽐标普500还便宜。我知道这听起来很离谱。它们的盈利在成倍增⻓，股

价却在往下⾛，所以市盈率越压越低。"
"市场认为AI会在2027年⻅顶。⽽且⼀种越来越成形的看法是，2028年会⾛平甚⾄下滑。"
"我认为市场对这⻔⽣意的融资能⼒做了过度修正。"
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⼗倍年增速与落地速度之间的裂缝
⽀撑 Kim 逆向判断的核⼼，是⼀个被他反复强调的概念：变化的速率。
他提到，随着"递归⾃我改进"这类机制出现，这条曲线的斜率还可能更陡。但真正的⽭盾在于另
⼀端：能⼒在⻜跃，采⽤在爬⾏。
这条裂缝正是他解释"看不到ROI"质疑的钥匙："⼤家说这项投资看不到回报，我认为它最终会显
现出来，只是也许不是现在，是明年、后年。"
同样的逻辑也解释了为什么他期待应⽤层。Kim 把AI拆成两层：⼀层是"原⼦"——电⼒、芯
⽚、服务器、云、超⼤⼚构成的计算与基础设施层；另⼀层是"电⼦"——软件与服务构成的应⽤
层。过去四年，全部故事都发⽣在第⼀层。
Agent 数量将百倍于⼈⼝，安全与数据是最先被点燃的环节
Kim 对应⽤层最先落地的⽅向，给出了⼀个反直觉的排序：⽹络安全。
他持仓中的 Okta 是典型案例。这家做⾝份安全的公司，核⼼业务是为⼈类提供⾝份认证，⼏年
前⾼增⻓，之后增速回落到个位数，"AI来了之后，2023到2025年这块核⼼业务并没有加速"。
转折发⽣在今年。

他给了⼀个量级对⽐：地球上有80亿⼈，⽽机器⾝份的数量最终可能是"上千亿"。
第⼆条线索是数据层。Kim ⽤⼀句话概括了他眼中未来企业的形态：
"模型能⼒⼤概⼀年提升⼀个数量级。如果算⼗年，就是⼀万倍；再⼗年，⼜是⼀万倍。这是⾮
线性的。"
"模型能⼒的增⻓速度，快于它在现实经济中的扩散速度。"
"这次Anthropic的上市，第⼀次出现⼀家纯粹的应⽤公司，也许它会开启AI的下⼀个阶段，让我
们⾛得⽐算⼒更宽。"
"现在有了Agent，它们不是⼈，是机器。今年出了这么多安全事件，从Hugging Face那次攻击
开始，⼤家意识到这些Agent可能会失控。所以你需要的不只是⼈类⾝份的安全，还有机器⾝
份、Agent⾝份的安全。"
"这个变化今年才刚刚开始，还处在极早期的阶段。"
"你会接⼊智能，选⼀个模型；中间是彭博、⻉莱德这样的数据层；上⾯跑着Agent。未来的公司
就⻓这样，Agent和你的数据交互，你往⾥灌原始智能。"

### Page 3

沿着这个图景，⻉莱德在数据管道、Agent构建、Agent防护三个环节上布了仓。他把这称作"企
业整体重构的第⼀局"。
99%的流量将由机器产⽣，⽽市场还在赌算⼒⻅顶
Kim 引⽤了 Cloudflare 的数据：今年年初，AI与机器流量已经超过⼈类流量。
他的推演是：每个⼈未来会有⼗个、甚⾄上百个Agent昼夜不停地⼯作。⼈类的刷⼿机流量在总
量⾥会变得微不⾜道，⽽更耗算⼒的是那些⾮⼈类的负载——药物研发、材料发现、化学与⽣
物学、机器⼈系统训练，"可能会有⼀整个数据中⼼只做药物发现"。
这正好回到他对市场定价的批评：
⾄于AI交易的新阶段怎么布局，Kim 的判断是软件、服务与AI模型将三合⼀，"以结果的形式交
付"。
他以⾃⼰的⼯作流举例：⽇历、邮件接⼊AI助⼿，每天处理两千封邮件、按优先级分流，再分设
多个Agent盯半导体、数据中⼼、基因组学，每周⽣成⼀份"AI在⽣物学领域的进展"⻓报告。

"如果今天机器和⼈类流量是六⽐四，五年后会是99⽐1。"
"越来越多的算⼒负载不是由⼈产⽣的，事实上99%都将是⾮⼈类的。"
"市场在说算⼒会在2028年⻅顶并下滑。这与即将到来的东西完全相反——所需的算⼒会⽐我们
现在设想的还要⾼出好⼏个数量级。这就是最⼤的分歧所在。"
"把AI模型、软件、服务这三样合成⼀个东西，以结果交付。然后把这个想法套⽤到标普的每⼀
个⾏业上。"
"现在还做不到让它替我把税报了，但⽅向是这样。"

### Page 4

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking
the cash rate after the November increase. Since then, our
conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we
will publish the November Market Outlook with revised
forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing

market have all been revised down in 2027.
Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for
calendar 2027 more decisively inside the RBA’s 2–3% target
range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment
rate forecast has been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on
this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash rate from
here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.

A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The
housing market has undershot our forecasts over the past
couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been
coming in under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan
inquiries have taken another leg down in October after the
September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior
months, with the additional impact of the November rate hike
still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer.

Consumer income and spending have so far been quite
resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest
rates. But our Westpac Card Tracker is starting to show that
discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite
weak. We would not over-emphasise this point, but it does
imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB)

meeting, business survey data has shown weaker conditions
and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show
further evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-
labour cost shock that is not being fully passed through to
consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil
and fuel prices are already materially lower than the track
assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-
fourth lower than on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts

were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of
fuel costs to other prices, as well as easing consumer inflation
perceptions and expectations.
An RBA peak of greater conviction
Forecast changes
• A full update to our forecasts has been published in our
November Market Outlook.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
In the media conference following the November meeting,
Governor Bullock flagged that it was a good time to “take

stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the
market read this as a signal that the MPB would pause at the
December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to
downside risks than the internal members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is
looking even less likely given signs in the data and surveys.
The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and

senior officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a
downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though,
a rate hike in 2027 would require either a material new
inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would
constitute a policy error on the part of the RBA (something
that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the talents
and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with

the dissenting voters in the MPB that the downside risks need
more attention.
Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to
February, we think there will be more evidence that the
domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation
outlook with it.
“A deeper housing downturn and early signs of
cracks in the consumer sector point to an even
softer 2027."

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the
passthrough of energy and technology input inflation. His
conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the monthly

rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward
supporting an increase in our policy rate of 25 basis points
at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI
inflation data out later today are similar to the past two
monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent
or even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

Document 316: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Document 329: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3 according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever-volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2-0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues. As anticipated by our Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2). 
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%). According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2), driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export growth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports (-1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q). Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, growth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026, 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate (AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheless 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI-related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

Figu

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

The Japan Research Institute, Limited- ８ -

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
FINANCE BULLETIN
Steep fall
11 November 2026
Key points
• The value of new dwelling finance
approvals excl. refinance dropped
15.5%qtr, the biggest quarterly decline
on records going back to 1991. Annual
growth swung to –18.1%yr. While the

decline was widely expected, it was
larger than the median forecast of -8.5%
and Westpac’s forecast of –10%.
• The quarterly drop was mostly driven
by lower volumes: total loan numbers
down 15%qtr (-21%yr). Smaller average
loan size contributed a further 0.5ppts
to the value decline in the quarter.
• Investors were the primary driver,
volumes in this segment down
over 30%qtr and the value of new
loans off by nearly a third.
• Borrowing by owner occupiers

significant but somewhat milder
declines, both the volume and value
of new loans down 5.3%qtr.
• More weakness looks likely for Q4.

finance:
–15.5%qtr,
–18.1%yr

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The value of new dwelling finance approvals fell steeply
in Q3, a 15%qtr drop to be down 25% year to date. The
quarterly fall was the largest since the series began in
1991 while the fall over the last nine months is on a par
with the 2022 downturn, which was the largest on record.

The outcome was significantly weaker than the consensus
forecast of an 8.5% drop. The downturn reflects the
combined impact of rising interest rates and major housing-
related tax policy changes announced at the May budget
which have hit investor loans for the purchase of existing
dwellings particularly hard.
The detail shows volumes were the main driver, the number
of new loans dropping 15%qtr with the average loan size
accounting for 0.5ppts of the quarterly decline in the total

value of new loans.
The segment split showed a 5.3%qtr fall in new owner
occupier loan approvals and a whopping 30% drop in new
investor loans. Within the latter, the number of investor
loans for the purchase of existing dwellings (which no longer
receive ‘negative gearing’ and capital gains tax concessions)
fell 35.6%qtr and are down by a half over the year to date. In
contrast, the number of investor loans for the construction or
purchase of newly built dwellings (which are still eligible for

concessions) declined 8.7%qtr and 8.5% year-to-date. This
segment now accounts for 27.6% of all investor loans, up from
17% late last year.
Owner-occupier loans reflected the wider influence of interest
rate and market uncertainty. The detail here shows a more
pronounced 8.6%qtr drop in first home buyer numbers, a more
interest rate sensitive segment. High construction costs also
look to be a factor with the number of owner occupier loans for
the construction and purchase of newly built dwellings down

11.9%qtr. Lending for land purchases and renovations also
declined 12.9%qtr in value terms.
Today’s results were weaker than expected with a bigger
pull-back in investor activity than we assessed when the tax
policy changes were announced back in May. Some of this may
be an overshoot with uncertainty about policy changes and
the wider price correction discouraging investors who might
otherwise have considered purchasing even without the tax
benefits. That said, conditions look far from conducive for even

a muted bounce near term. More weak reads look likely for the
final quarter of 2026.
